0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.44803pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varphi_{h}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.39679pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.39679pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.98013pt\raise-16.49501pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.89168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{c_{1}N}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.98013pt\raise-25.65671pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-17.39679pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Fil^{h}\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.44803pt\raise-27.80252pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varphi_{h}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.39679pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.39679pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{M}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

Remark 6.18
.

Breuil and Caruso use convention
N_{S}(u)=-u
. In fact, there is almost no difference for entire theory by using
N_{S}(u)=u
except for the formula (
6.20
) need to change sign comparing with the similar formula
[
Liu08
, (5.1.1) ]

Let
\Mod_{S,\tor}^{\varphi,h,N}
denote the category of Breuil modules. There is a natural functor
\Mod_{S,\tor}^{\varphi,h,\nabla}\to\Mod_{S,\tor}^{\varphi,h,N}
by define
N_{\mathcal{M}}=u\nabla
. It is easy to chase the diagram to see this functor makes sense. So we also call objects in
\textnormal{Mod}_{S,\tor}^{\varphi,h,\nabla}
Breuil modules.

Now we can define a
G_{K}
-action on
\mathcal{M}\otimes_{S}A_{\cris}
as in:
for any
\sigma\in G_{K}
,
any
x\otimes a\in\mathcal{M}\otimes_{S}A_{\cris}
,
define

(6.19)

\sigma(x\otimes a)=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\nabla^{i}(x)\otimes\gamma_{i}\left(\sigma([\underline{\pi}])-[\underline{\pi}]\right)\sigma(a).

We can also define a
G_{K}
-action on
\mathcal{M}\otimes_{S}A_{\cris}
as in
[
Liu10
, §5.1]
:
for any
\sigma\in G_{K}
, recall
\underline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)=\frac{\sigma([\underline{\pi}])}{[\underline{\pi}]}\in A_{\inf}
. For
any
x\otimes a\in\mathcal{M}\otimes_{S}A_{\cris}
,
define

(6.20)

\sigma(x\otimes a)=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}N^{i}(x)\otimes\gamma_{i}(\log(\underline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)))\sigma(a).

where
\gamma_{i}(x)=\frac{x^{i}}{i!}
is the standard divided power. We claim that (
6.19
) and (
6.20
) are the same formula. Let us postpone the proof in §
8.1
as the proof is just long combinatoric calculation.

Note that if
\sigma\in G_{\infty}
, then
\log(\underline{\varepsilon}(\sigma))=0
and

\sigma(x\otimes a)=x\otimes\sigma(a)
. Thus
G_{K}
-action defined
above (if it is well defined) is compatible with the natural

G_{\infty}
-action on
\mathcal{M}\otimes_{S}A_{\cris}
.

Lemma 6.21
.

The above action is well defined
A_{\cris}
-semi-linear
G_{K}
-action on
\mathcal{M}\otimes_{S}A_{\cris}
and compatible with
\Fil^{h}(\mathcal{M}\otimes_{S}A_{\cris})
and
\varphi_{h}
.

Proof.

The proof of
[
Liu10
, §5.1]
essentially applies here. It is standard to check that (
6.19
) is well-defined map; it is
A_{\cris}
-semi-linear-action on
\mathcal{M}\otimes_{S}A_{\cris}
and compatible with
G_{K}
-action on
A_{\cris}
; and
G_{\infty}
-acts on
\mathcal{M}\otimes 1
trivially. It is clear that
\log(\underline{\varepsilon}(\sigma))\in\Fil^{1}A_{\cris}
. So by that
E(u)N(\Fil^{h}\mathcal{M})\subset\Fil^{h}\mathcal{M}
, we see that

\sigma(\Fil^{h}(\mathcal{M}\otimes_{S}A_{\cris}))\subset\Fil^{h}(\mathcal{M}\otimes_{S}A_{\cris})
. The only thing left to check is that
\varphi_{h}
commutes with
G_{K}
-action, which can be reduce to check the following: write
a=-\log(\underline{\varepsilon}(\sigma))
and pick
x\in\Fil^{h}\mathcal{M}
, we have

\varphi_{h}(\gamma_{i}(a)\otimes N^{i}(x))=\gamma_{i}(a)\otimes N^{i}(\varphi_{h}(x)).

It is clear that
\varphi(a)=pa
. So
\varphi(\gamma_{i}(a))=\gamma_{i}(a)c_{1}^{-i}\varphi(E(u)^{i})
. So the above equality is reduced to check

c_{1}^{-i}\varphi_{h}(E(u)^{i}N^{i}(x))=N^{i}(\varphi_{h}(x))
and this can be check by induction on
i
.
∎

Corollary 6.22
.

Given a Breuil module
\mathcal{M}\in\textnormal{Mod}^{\varphi,h,N}_{S,\tor}
,
then
T_{S}(\mathcal{M})
(as a
G_{\infty}
-representation) extends to a
G_{K}
-representation.

To summarize our section, we return to the situation of §
5.2
where
\mathcal{M}^{i}:={\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
is proved to admit structures
\Fil^{i}\mathcal{M}^{i}={\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n},\mathcal{I}^{[i]}_{\cris}),

\varphi_{i}:\Fil^{i}\mathcal{M}^{i}\to\mathcal{M}^{i}
and
\nabla:\mathcal{M}^{i}\to\mathcal{M}^{i}
. Obviously, our axioms of
\textnormal{Mod}^{\varphi,h,\nabla}_{S,\tor}
is aimed at describing these structures of
{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
.

Definition 6.23
.

For
i\leq p-2
, we call that
{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
is a Breuil module if the quadruple

\left({\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n}),{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n},\mathcal{I}^{[i]}_{\cris}),\varphi_{i},\nabla\right)

constructed in §
5.2
is an object in
\Mod_{S,\tor}^{\varphi,i,\nabla}
, which is equivalent to the triple

\left({\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n}),{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n},\mathcal{I}^{[i]}_{\cris}),\varphi_{i}\right)

being an object in
\Mod_{S,\tor}^{\varphi,i}
.

Our main theorem is to show that
{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
together with these structures is indeed a Breuil module when
ei<p-1
.

7.
Torsion cohomology and comparison with étale cohomology

In this section, we collect our previous preparations to understand the structures of torsion crystalline cohomology and its relationship with étale cohomology via torsion prismatic cohomology. In the end, we
show that if
ei<p-1
then
p^{n}
-th torsion crystalline cohomology
{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
has structure of torsion Breuil module to compare to
{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})
via
T_{S}
, where
X_{\overline{\eta}}
is a geometric generic fiber of
X
.

7.1.
Prismatic cohomology and (generalized) Kisin modules

Let
(A,I)
be any prism. As in the end of §
5.2
,
for any
n\geq 1
, we define torsion prismatic cohomology
\mathrm{R\Gamma}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/A_{n}):=\mathrm{R\Gamma}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X/A,\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})=\RG_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X/A)\otimes^{\mathbb L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
. We have

\RG_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/A_{n})\simeq\RG_{\qsyn}(X,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{-/A}/p^{n})\simeq\RG_{\qsyn}(X,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{-/A})\otimes^{\mathbb L}{Z}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
.

Warning 7.1
.

We warn readers that the notation
\mathrm{R\Gamma}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/A_{n})
is misleading, as it might suggest that this cohomology
theory only depends on the mod
p^{n}
reduction of
X
which is not true.
See
[
BMS18
, Remark 2.4]
for a counterexample.

Proposition 7.2
.

Assume that
(A,I)
is transversal and
\varphi:A\to A
is flat. Then
{\rm H}^{i}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/A_{n})
has height
i
.

Proof.

We follow the same idea of

[
BS19
, Corollary 15.5]
which proved that
{\rm H}^{i}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X/A)
has height
i
.
Examining the proof, it suffices to show that
\varphi^{*}\RG_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{n}/A_{n})\simeq L\eta_{I}\RG_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{n}/A_{n})
when
X=\Spf(R)
is an affine smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
A/I
.
By Theorem 15.3 of
loc. cit
, we have

\varphi^{*}\RG_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X/A)\simeq L\eta_{I}\RG_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X/A)
. Since
\varphi:A\to A
is flat, it suffices to show that

(7.3)

\left(L\eta_{I}\RG_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X/A)\right)\otimes^{{\mathbb L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}\simeq L\eta_{I}\left(\RG_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X/A)\otimes^{{\mathbb L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}\right).

Now we may apply
[
Bha18b
, Lemma 5.16]
to the above by
g=p^{n}
and
f=d
. So we need to check that
{\rm H}^{*}(\RG_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X/A)\otimes^{{\mathbb L}}_{A}A/d)
has no
p^{n}
-torsion. This follows from the Hodge–Tate comparison

{\rm H}^{i}(\RG_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X/A)\otimes^{{\mathbb L}}_{A}A/I)\simeq\Omega^{i}_{X/(A/I)}\{i\}.

∎

Corollary 7.4
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, the
\varphi
-module
{\rm H}^{i}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})
is an object of
\Mod_{\mathfrak{S}}^{\varphi,i}
, i.e.,
a (generalized) Kisin module of height
i
and
T_{\mathfrak{S}}({\rm H}^{i}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}))\simeq{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that
T_{\mathfrak{S}}({\rm H}^{i}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}))\simeq{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})
. Write
\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}:={\rm H}^{i}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})
,
{\mathcal{X}}\coloneqq\Spf\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{C}}\times_{\Spf\mathcal{O}_{K}}X
.
For
n\not=\infty
, by
[
BS19
, Theorem 1.8 (4) (5)]
, we have

{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})\simeq\left({\rm H}^{i}(\mathrm{R\Gamma}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}({\mathcal{X}}/A_{\inf})/p^{n})[\frac{1}{E(u)}]\right)^{\varphi=1}=(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}W_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\flat})[\frac{1}{u}])^{\varphi=1}=(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}W_{n}(\mathbf{C}^{\flat}))^{\varphi=1},

which is just
T_{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})
. The case of
n=\infty
easily follows by taking inverse limits.
∎

Remark 7.5
.

The
G_{\infty}
-action on
T_{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})
discussed in §
6.2
naturally extends to a
G_{K}
-action by isomorphism
\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}A_{\inf}\simeq{\rm H}^{i}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}({\mathcal{X}}/A_{\inf})
, which admits a natural
G_{K}
-action that commutes with
\varphi
. In this way
T_{\mathfrak{S}}({\rm H}^{i}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}))\simeq{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})
is an isomorphism of
G_{K}
-actions.

Let
X_{k}:=X\times_{\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{K})}\Spf(k)
be the closed fiber of
X
.

Lemma 7.6
.

If
\Len_{W(k)}{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{k}/W_{n}(k))=\Len_{\mathbb{Z}}{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})
then
\mathfrak{M}^{j}_{n}
has no
u
-torsion for
j=i,i+1
.

Proof.

We claim that
\RG_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})\otimes^{\mathbb L}_{\mathfrak{S}}W(k)\simeq\RG_{\cris}(X_{k}/W_{n}(k)).
To see this, first note that
(\mathfrak{S},E)\to(W(k),p)
by mod
u
is a map of prisms. So
[
BS19
, Theorem 1.8 (5)]
proves that
\RG_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X/\mathfrak{S})\otimes^{\mathbb L}_{\mathfrak{S}}W(k)\simeq\RG_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{k}/W(k))
. Then Theorem 1.8 (1)
loc. cit.
shows that
\RG_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X/\mathfrak{S})\otimes^{\mathbb L}_{\mathfrak{S}}W(k)\simeq\RG_{\cris}(X_{k}/W(k))
. Then the claim follows by
\otimes^{\mathbb L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
on both sides.

The claim immediately shows that the exact sequence

(7.7)

0\to\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}/u\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\to{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{k}/W_{n}(k))\to\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n}[u]\to 0

So
\Len_{W(k)}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}/u\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\leq\Len_{W(k)}{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{k}/W_{n}(k))
. On the other hand, consider the exact sequence in Lemma
6.2
with
\mathfrak{M}:=\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}

0\to\mathfrak{M}[u^{\infty}]\to\mathfrak{M}\to\mathfrak{M}/\mathfrak{M}[u^{\infty}]\to 0

Write
\mathfrak{M}^{\et}:=\mathfrak{M}/\mathfrak{M}[u^{\infty}]
. Since
\mathfrak{M}^{\et}
has no
u
-torsion, the above exact sequence remaining exact by modulo
u
. So we have

\Len_{W(k)}(\mathfrak{M}^{\et}/u\mathfrak{M}^{\et})\leq\Len_{W(k)}\mathfrak{M}/u\mathfrak{M}
and equality holds only when
\mathfrak{M}[u^{\infty}]=\{0\}
. Since
T_{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M})=T_{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M}^{\et})
, and
T_{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M})\simeq{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})
by Corollary
7.4
, Corollary
6.15
proves the following inequalities

\Len_{\mathbb{Z}}{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})=\Len_{W(k)}(\mathfrak{M}^{\et}/u\mathfrak{M}^{\et})\leq\Len_{W(k)}(\mathfrak{M}/u\mathfrak{M}).

Now combine with the exact sequence (
7.7
), we conclude that

\Len_{\mathbb{Z}}{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})\leq\Len_{W(k)}{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{k}/W_{n}(k))

and equality holds only if all the above inequalities become equalities and
\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}
and
\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n}
have no
u
-torsions.
∎

7.2.
Nygaard filtration and Breuil–Kisin filtration

By Corollary
7.4
,
\mathfrak{M}_{n}^{i}:={\rm H}^{i}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})
is a Kisin module of height
i
. Then
\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\simeq{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{-/\mathfrak{S}}\otimes^{\mathbb L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})
admits two filtrations: Breuil–Kisin filtration defined in (
6.8
) and Nygaard filtration
{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X,\Fil^{i}_{\rm N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{-/\mathfrak{S}}\otimes^{\mathbb L}_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}})
. The aim of this subsection is to compare these two filtrations.

This theme can be put in more general setting for a bounded prism
(A,I)
. Recall that in
[
BS19
, §15]
the authors studied

{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{-/A}
and
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{-/A}:=A\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathbb L}}_{\varphi,A}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{-/A}
as sheaves on
\mathrm{qSyn}_{A/I}
.
Also constructed in
loc. cit.
is the so-called Nygaard filtration
\Fil^{j}_{\rm N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{-/A}
, also discussed §
4.2
.
For any
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, set
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{n}:={{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{-/A}\otimes^{{\mathbb L}}{Z}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
and

\Fil^{j}_{\rm N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{n}:=\Fil^{j}_{\rm N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{-/A}\otimes^{{\mathbb L}}{Z}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
.
Here and below, we adopt the convention that
n=\infty
means we do not perform any base change.

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
(A,I)
be a bounded prism.
Let
X
be a smooth (
p
-adic) formal scheme over
\Spf(A/I)
of relative dimension
n
.
Then we have:

(1)

The Nygaard filtration
\mathrm{R\Gamma}(X_{\qsyn},\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{N}})
on
\mathrm{R\Gamma}(X_{\qsyn},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{-/A})

is complete.

(2)

The natural map

\Fil^{i}_{\mathrm{N}}\otimes_{A}I^{j}\to\Fil^{i+j}_{\mathrm{N}}

of quasisyntomic sheaves induces a morphism

{\rm H}^{l}(X_{\qsyn},\Fil^{i}_{\mathrm{N}})\otimes_{A}I^{j}\rightarrow{\rm H}^{l}(X_{\qsyn},\Fil^{i+j}_{\mathrm{N}})

which is an isomorphism when either
l\leq i
, and an injection when
l=i+1
.
When
i\geq n
this map induces an isomorphism

\mathrm{R\Gamma}(X_{\qsyn},\Fil^{n}_{\mathrm{N}})\otimes_{A}I^{j}\cong\mathrm{R\Gamma}(X_{\qsyn},\Fil^{n+j}_{\mathrm{N}}).

(3)

The natural map

\varphi\colon\Fil^{i}_{\mathrm{N}}\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{-/A}\otimes_{A}I^{i}

induces a map on cohomology

{\rm H}^{l}(X_{\qsyn},\Fil^{i}_{\mathrm{N}})\to{\rm H}^{l}(X_{\qsyn},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{-/A})\otimes_{A}I^{i}

which is an isomorphism when
l\leq i

and injective when
l=i+1
.

Moreover their derived mod
p^{m}
counterparts hold true as well.

We thank Bhargav for pointing out the statement (3) above, which we did not realize can be proved so easily.
This significantly simplifies an earlier draft.

Proof.

(1) follows from (2). Indeed, (2) implies the Nygaard filtration on

\mathrm{R\Gamma}(X_{\qsyn},\Fil^{i}_{\mathrm{N}})
is simply the
I
-adic filtration,
hence it is complete.

(2) follows from the following exact triangle of quasisyntomic sheaves:

\Fil^{i}_{\mathrm{N}}\otimes_{A}I\to\Fil^{i+1}_{\mathrm{N}}\to\Fil^{i+1}_{\mathrm{H}}\dR_{-/(A/I)}^{\wedge}.

Observe that

\mathrm{R\Gamma}(X_{\qsyn},\Fil^{l}_{\mathrm{H}}\dR_{-/(A/I)}^{\wedge})\cong\mathrm{R\Gamma}(X,\Fil^{l}_{\mathrm{H}}\dR_{-/(A/I)}^{\wedge})

lives in
D^{\geq l}(A/I)
,
and vanishes when
l>n
.
An easy induction gives what we want.

As for (3): we look at the map of filtered complexes

\mathrm{R\Gamma}(X_{\qsyn},\Fil^{i}_{\mathrm{N}})\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathrm{R\Gamma}(X_{\qsyn},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{-/A}\otimes_{A}I^{i})

where the former is equipped with Nygaard filtration
\mathrm{R\Gamma}(X_{\qsyn},\Fil^{i+\ast}_{\mathrm{N}})

and the latter is equipped with
I
-adic filtration
\mathrm{R\Gamma}(X_{\qsyn},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{-/A}\otimes_{A}I^{i+\ast})
.
Notice that both filtrations are complete.
Now
[
BS19
, Theorem 15.2.(2)]
implies that the cone of the
(i+\ast)
-th graded piece lives in
D^{>(i+\ast)}(A/I)
.
Hence we conclude that the cone of
\varphi
lives in
D^{>i}(A)
.
Therefore the induced maps of degree at most
i
cohomology groups are isomorphisms,
and the induced map in degree
i+1
is injective.

Their derived mod
p^{m}
counterparts are proved in exactly the same way.
∎

Now let us return to the situation of Breuil–Kisin prism
A=\mathfrak{S}
.
Recall that
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{n}:={{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{-/\mathfrak{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
and
\Fil^{i}_{\rm N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{n}:=\Fil^{i}_{\rm N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{-/\mathfrak{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
.
Recall that
\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\coloneqq{\rm H}^{i}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})

and recall that Breuil–Kisin-filtration on
\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\cong{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{n})
is defined as the image of

\psi\colon\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\to\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}
.

Corollary 7.9
.

For any
i\in\mathbb{N}
and any
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, there is a functorial commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X,\Fil^{i}_{\rm N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{i}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}}

with
\varphi_{i}
an isomorphism.

Proof.

First let us justify the existence of the functorial commutative diagram.
We may work with affine formal schemes
Y=\Spf(R)
.
In this case, by the proof of
[
BS19
, Theorem 15.3 and Corollary 15.5]
,
we see
\psi
is constructed by the following (right-lower corner) diagram

\textstyle{\tau^{\leq i}\RG_{\qsyn}(Y,\Fil^{i}_{\rm N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau^{\leq i}\RG_{\qsyn}(Y,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\otimes(E)^{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\tau^{\leq i}\RG_{\qsyn}(Y,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
-
\crvi
-
\crvi
-
\crvi
-
\crvi
-
\crvi
-
\crvi
-
\crvi
-
\crvi
-
\crvi
-
\crvi
-
\crvi
-
\crvi

\textstyle{\tau^{\leq i}\RG_{\qsyn}(Y,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})}

Here the top row is the same as (truncation by
\leq i
of) the following morphism

\mathrm{R\Gamma}(X_{\qsyn},\Fil^{i}_{\mathrm{N}})\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathrm{R\Gamma}(X_{\qsyn},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{-/A}\otimes_{A}I^{i})

appeared in Lemma
7.8
.
Derived mod

gives the desired functorial commutative diagram.
By

(3) we know
that
\varphi_{i}
is an isomorphism.
∎

Remark 7.10
.

In the context of filtered derived infinity categories, a filtration is nothing but an arrow.
Hence one could define two ‘‘quasi-filtrations’’
3
3

3

This terminology is suggested by S. Mondal.
:
one being the Breuil–Kisin quasi-filtration:
\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\xrightarrow{\psi}\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}
;
another being the
i
-th Nygaard quasi-filtration:
{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X,\Fil^{i}_{\rm N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{n})\to\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}
.
Then the above is saying that these two quasi-filtrations are canonically identified via
\varphi_{i}
.

Let us name the map

\iota^{i,j}_{n}\colon{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X,\Fil^{j}_{\rm N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{n})\to\Fil^{j}_{\BK}{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{n})

for any pair of natural numbers
(i,j)
and any
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.
We have the following knowledge of the image of
\iota^{i,j}_{n}
when
i\leq j
.

Corollary 7.11
.

Let
i\leq j
.
Then we have an identification

\mathrm{Im}(\iota^{i,j}_{n})\cong\mathrm{Im}(\psi\colon\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\to\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})\cdot E^{j-i}.

In particular, define
\widetilde{\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}}\coloneqq\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}/[u^{\infty}]
and
\widetilde{\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}}\coloneqq\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}/[u^{\infty}]
,
we have an identification

\mathrm{Im}(\widetilde{\iota}^{i,j}_{n}\colon{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X,\Fil^{i}_{\rm N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{n})\to\Fil^{i}_{\BK}\widetilde{\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}})\cong\{x\in\widetilde{\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}}|(1\otimes\varphi)(x)\in E(u)^{j}\widetilde{\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}}\}.

Proof.

The first statement follows from combining
Lemma

7.8
(2) and
Corollary

7.9
.
The second statement follows from the first statement and the fact that
\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}
has height
i
.
∎

Below we make some primitive investigations of what happens without assuming
i\leq j
.

Proposition 7.12
.

Let
A=\mathfrak{S}
be the Breuil–Kisin prism.
For any triple
(i,j,n)
, the kernel and cokernel of
\iota^{i,j}_{n}
above are finite.

Proof.

Note that the kernel and cokernel of
\iota^{i,j}_{n}
are finitely generated modules over
\mathfrak{S}/(p^{n})
.
We have a containment

E(u)^{j}\cdot{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}\subset\Fil^{j}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}\subset{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}

of sheaves on
\mathrm{qSyn}_{A/I}
.
This shows that the map
\iota^{i,j}_{n}
admits a section up to multiplication by
E(u)^{j}
,
therefore the kernel and cokernel of
\iota^{i,j}_{n}
are annihilated by
E(u)^{j}
.
If
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the kernel and cokernel of
\iota^{i,j}_{n}
are finitely generated modules over
\mathfrak{S}/(p^{n},E(u)^{j})
, hence finite.

If
n=\infty
, denote the map by
\iota^{i,j}
, we make the following

Claim 7.13
.

The map
\iota^{i,j}\colon{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X,\Fil^{j}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})[1/p]\to\Fil^{j}_{\BK}\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}^{i}[1/p]
is an isomorphism.

Granting this claim, the kernel and cokernel of
\iota^{i,j}
are finitely generated modules over
\mathfrak{S}/(E(u)^{j})
annihilated by a power
of
p
, hence finite.
∎

Proof of
7.13
.

First let us show the injectivity, which is the same as injectivity of

{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X,\Fil^{j}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})[1/p]\to{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})[1/p].

To this end, we use the filtration
\Fil^{i,j}
discussed in
Section

4.2
.
We claim a slightly stronger statement: the maps

{\rm H}^{m}_{\qsyn}(X,\Fil^{i,j}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})[1/p]\to{\rm H}^{m}_{\qsyn}(X,\Fil^{i,0}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})[1/p]

are injective for all
i\geq 0
.
The case of
i\geq j
is trivial due to
Proposition

4.10
(2).
For the rest of
i
, we perform induction on descending
i
.
By five Lemma and
Proposition

4.10
(3), it suffices to know that the maps

{\rm H}^{m}(X,\Fil^{j-i}_{\mathrm{H}}\dR_{X/\mathcal{O}_{K}})[1/p]\to{\rm H}^{m}(X,\dR_{X/\mathcal{O}_{K}})[1/p]

are injective.
This injectivity is equivalent to the degeneration of the Hodge-to-de Rham spectral sequence for the rigid space
X_{K}
, which is a result
due to Scholze
[
Sch13
, Theorem 1.8]
.

Next we show surjectivity by induction on
j
, the case of
j=0
being trivial. All we need to show is that the induced map

\mathrm{Coker}\bigg({\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X,\Fil^{j+1}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})[1/p]\to{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X,\Fil^{j}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})[1/p]\bigg)\xrightarrow{\overline{\varphi}}\frac{E(u)^{j}\mathfrak{M}^{i}}{E(u)^{j+1}\mathfrak{M}^{i}}[1/p]

is injective.
By the injectivity of
\iota^{i,j}[1/p]
proved in the previous paragraph,
we can rewrite the left hand side as
{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X,\gr^{j}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})[1/p]
.
Recall that
\mathfrak{M}^{i}[1/p]
is finite free over
\mathfrak{S}[1/p]
(see
Lemma

6.1
(3)),
therefore the right hand side can be rewritten as

{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X,\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})[1/p]\{j\}
, the
j
-th Breuil–Kisin twist
of the
i
-th Hodge–Tate cohomology of
X_{K}
.
By
[
BS19
, Theorem 15.2]
, we can identify the left hand side
further as the
j
-th conjugate filtration of the right hand side.
Now it follows from the degeneration of Hodge–Tate spectral sequence
[
BMS18
, Theorem 13.3]
that
\overline{\varphi}
is always injective.
∎

Below we exhibit an example illustrating the necessity of the
i\leq j
assumption in
Corollary

7.11
.

Example 7.14

(see
[
Li20
, Section 4]
)
.

Let
K
be a ramified quadratic extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let
G
be a lift of
\alpha_{p}
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Denote the classifying stack of
G
by
BG
.
Below we summarize previous study of various cohomologies of
BG
as documented in
[
Li20
, 4.6-4.10]
, following notation thereof.

(1)

The Breuil–Kisin prismatic cohomology ring of
BG
is given by

{\rm H}^{*}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(BG/\mathfrak{S})\cong\mathfrak{S}[\widetilde{u}]/(p\cdot\widetilde{u})

where
\widetilde{u}
has degree
2
.

(2)

The Hodge–Tate spectral sequence does not degenerate at
E_{2}
page, but does degenerate at
E_{3}
page, giving rise
to short exact sequences:

0\to{\rm H}^{i+1}(BG,\wedge^{i-1}\mathbb{L}_{BG/\mathcal{O}_{K}})\simeq\mathbb{F}_{p}\to{\rm H}^{2i}_{\mathrm{HT}}(BG/\mathcal{O}_{K})\simeq\mathcal{O}_{K}/(p)\to{\rm H}^{i}(BG,\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{BG/\mathcal{O}_{K}})\simeq\mathbb{F}_{p}\to 0

for all
i>0
.

(3)

The Hodge-to-de Rham spectral sequence does not degenerate at
E_{1}
page, but does degenerate at
E_{2}
page, giving rise
to short exact sequences:

0\to{\rm H}^{2i-1}(BG,\mathbb{L}_{BG/\mathcal{O}_{K}})\simeq\mathbb{F}_{p}\to{\rm H}^{2i}_{\mathrm{dR}}(BG/\mathcal{O}_{K})\simeq\mathcal{O}_{K}/(p)\to{\rm H}^{2i}(BG,\mathcal{O}_{BG})\simeq\mathbb{F}_{p}\to 0

for all
i>0
.

By
[
BS19
, Theorem 15.2]
, we have the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathrm{R\Gamma}_{\qsyn}(BG/\mathfrak{S},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R\Gamma}_{\qsyn}(BG/\mathfrak{S},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R\Gamma_{dR}}(BG/\mathcal{O}_{K})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R\Gamma}(BG,\mathcal{O}_{BG})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{R\Gamma_{HT}}(BG/\mathcal{O}_{K})}

where
\varphi
is the Frobenius on prismatic cohomology, vertical maps are derived modulo
E(u)
reductions, the two arrows
on the bottom row are natural arrows appearing in Hodge-to-de Rham and Hodge–Tate spectral sequences respectively.
Looking at the degree
2
cohomology together with (2) and (3) above, we see that
\varphi
on
{\rm H}^{2}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(BG/\mathfrak{S})

is given by, up to a unit in
\mathfrak{S}/p
, multiplication by
u\in\mathfrak{S}/p
.
Since
\varphi
is a map of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras, using (1) we see that
\varphi
on
{\rm H}^{4}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(BG/\mathfrak{S})

is given by, up to a unit in
\mathfrak{S}/p
, multiplication by
u^{2}=E(u)\in\mathfrak{S}/p
.
In particular, we see that
\Fil^{1}_{\BK}{\rm H}^{4}_{\qsyn}(BG/\mathfrak{S},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})={\rm H}^{4}_{\qsyn}(BG/\mathfrak{S},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})
is the whole cohomology group.

On the other hand, we claim that the map

{\rm H}^{4}_{\qsyn}(BG/\mathfrak{S},\Fil^{1}_{\mathrm{N}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})\to{\rm H}^{4}_{\qsyn}(BG/\mathfrak{S},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})

is not surjective.
Indeed we have a long exact sequence coming from the exact triangle
\Fil^{1}_{\mathrm{N}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}\to\mathcal{O}_{BG}

with the second arrow being the composition of derived modulo
E(u)
followed by projection modulo first Hodge filtration.
Hence (3) above shows that the cokernel is exactly of length
1
.
This shows that
BG
is a smooth proper stack counterexample for
(i,j,n)=(4,1,\infty)
.
Since all these cohomology groups are
p
-torsion, we see that this also provides a stacky
counterexample for
(i,j,n)=(3,1,1)
.

Finally let us use an approximation of
BG
to get a smooth proper scheme counterexample.
By
[
Li20
, Subsection 4.3]
there is a smooth projective fourfold
X
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
together with a map

f\colon X\to BG
such that the induced pullback map of Hodge cohomology is injective when total degree is no larger than
4
.
By functoriality of the formation of Breuil–Kisin filtrations,
we know that

\mathrm{Im}\bigg(f^{*}\colon{\rm H}^{4}_{\qsyn}(BG/\mathfrak{S},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})\to{\rm H}^{4}_{\qsyn}(X/\mathfrak{S},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})\bigg)\subset\Fil^{1}_{\BK}{\rm H}^{4}_{\qsyn}(X/\mathfrak{S},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}).

Lastly we claim that
f^{*}(\widetilde{u}^{2})\in{\rm H}^{4}_{\qsyn}(X/\mathfrak{S},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})
is not in the image of
{\rm H}^{4}_{\qsyn}(X/\mathfrak{S},\Fil^{1}_{\mathrm{N}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})
.
To see this it suffices to compare the two exact sequences:

\textstyle{{\rm H}^{4}_{\qsyn}(BG/\mathfrak{S},\Fil^{1}_{\mathrm{N}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{4}_{\qsyn}(BG/\mathfrak{S},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{4}_{\qsyn}(BG,\mathcal{O}_{BG})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{4}_{\qsyn}(X/\mathfrak{S},\Fil^{1}_{\mathrm{N}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{4}_{\qsyn}(X/\mathfrak{S},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{4}_{\qsyn}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})}

and invoke the fact that
f^{*}
is injective by our choice of
X
.
This gives us smooth projective fourfold over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
violating the conclusion of
Corollary

7.11

for
(i,j,n)=(4,1,\infty)
or
(i,j,n)=(3,1,1)
.

7.3.
Torsion crystalline cohomology

Now we are ready to discuss the structure of
{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
via prismatic cohomology.
First, we provide an application of the comparison

\mathrm{R\Gamma}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathfrak{S})\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\varphi}S\cong\mathrm{R\Gamma}_{\cris}(\mathcal{X}/S)
,
which concerns the module structure of the cohomology of the latter.
We need some preparations.

Lemma 7.15
.

The rings
S/p^{n}
are coherent for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

We do not know if the ring
S
itself is coherent.

Proof.

We make an induction on
n
.
The starting case
n=1
: since
S
is given by
p
-completely
adjoin the divided powers of the Eisenstein polynomial
E(u)
to
\mathfrak{S}
,
we see that
S/p
is obtained by adjoining divided powers of
E(u)\equiv u^{e}

to
\mathfrak{S}/p=k[\![u]\!]
.
It is well-known that the result is

S/p\cong k[u]/u^{pe}\otimes_{k}k[u_{1},u_{2},\ldots]/(u_{i}^{p})

where
u_{i}
is the image of the
p^{i}
-th divided powers of
E(u)
.
One checks this explicit algebra is coherent by noting
that any finitely generated ideal is generated by polynomials
involving only finitely many variables.

Now we do the induction, which largely relies on
[
BMS18
, Lemma 3.26]
.
Indeed the cited Lemma reduces us to showing the ideal
(p^{n})/(p^{n+1})

in
S/p^{n+1}
, when viewed as an
S/p
-module, is finitely presented.
But in fact
S
is
p
-torsion free, hence the ideal

(p^{n})/(p^{n+1})
is free when viewed as an
S/p
-module with generator
p^{n}
.
∎

Lemma 7.16
.

Suppose that
C^{\bullet}
is a prefect

\mathfrak{S}_{n}
-complex.
Then there exists an exact sequence of
S
-modules

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}(C^{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}(C^{\bullet}\otimes^{\mathbb L}_{\mathfrak{S}}S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Tor_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}({\rm H}^{i+1}(C^{\bullet}),S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

In particular
S
has Tor-amplitude
1
over
\mathfrak{S}
and the functor
M\mapsto\Tor_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(M,S)
is left exact.

Proof.

For the first claim, see the argument before the proof of Theorem 5.4 in
[
CL19
]
and replace
A_{\inf}
(resp.
A_{\cris}
) there by
\mathfrak{S}
(resp.
S
).
The fact that
S
has Tor-amplitude
1
over
\mathfrak{S}
follows from the Auslander–Buchsbaum formula
and torsion-freeness of
S
.
∎

Proposition 7.17
.

Let
M
be a finitely generated Kisin module, then

\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(M,\varphi_{*}S)

is a finitely presented
S
module.

Proof.

Denote
N\coloneqq M[u^{\infty}]
which is the maximal finite length
\mathfrak{S}
-submodule
inside
M
.

We first show
\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(N,\varphi_{*}S)\to\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(M,\varphi_{*}S)

is an isomorphism.
Since
S
has Tor-amplitude
1
over
\mathfrak{S}
by
Lemma

7.16
,
it suffices to show the vanishing of
\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(M/N,\varphi_{*}S)
.
Note that
M/N
is an étale Kisin module, we have a sequence

0\to(M/N)_{\mathrm{tor}}\to M/N\to(M/N)_{\mathrm{tf}}\to 0,

where
(M/N)_{\mathrm{tor}}
is a successive extension of
k[\![u]\!]=\mathfrak{S}/p

as
M/N
is étale, and
(M/N)_{\mathrm{tf}}
is torsion-free.
Next observe that both these two structures are preserved under
base change along the Frobenius on
\mathfrak{S}
.
Therefore it suffices to show
\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(-,S)=0

whenever the inputting
\mathfrak{S}
-module is
\mathfrak{S}/p

or torsion-free. For the former case, it follows from the fact that
S

has no
p
-torsion.
For the latter, consider the reflexive hull
M^{\prime\vee\vee}
of the inputting module

M^{\prime}\subset M^{\prime\vee\vee}
, which is finite free as
\mathfrak{S}

is a regular Noetherian of dimension
2
.
Finally the desired vanishing of
\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(M^{\prime},S)

follows from the left exactness of
\mathrm{Tor}_{1}
against
S
over
\mathfrak{S}
:
see
Lemma

7.16
.

It suffices to show
\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(N^{\prime},S)
is finitely presented
for any finite length
\mathfrak{S}
-module,
which is the content of the next lemma.
∎

Lemma 7.18
.

Let
N
be a finite length
\mathfrak{S}
-module, then

N\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}S
and

\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(N,S)
are finitely presented
S
-modules.

Proof.

When
N=k\cong\mathfrak{S}/(p,u)
, the statement for

k\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}S=S/(p,u)=(S/p)/u
and

\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(k,S)\cong S/p[u]
follows from the fact that

S/p
is coherent (
Lemma

7.15
) and
[
Sta20
,
Tag 05CW (3)
]
.

Next we make an induction on the length of
N
.
By considering
N\twoheadrightarrow N/(p,u)\simeq k^{\oplus r}
,
we have a short exact sequence

0\to N^{\prime}\to N\to k\to 0
, which induces a long exact sequence:

0\to\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(N^{\prime},S)\to\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(N,S)\to\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(k,S)\to N^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}S\to N\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}S\to k\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}S\to 0.

Induction hypotheses imply that all terms except of

N\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}S
and

\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(N,S)
are finitely presented
S
-modules.
Note that the finite length assumption implies all modules are
S/p^{N}
-modules
for some sufficiently large
N
.
The coherence of
S/p^{N}
(
Lemma

7.15
) and
[
Sta20
,
Tag 05CW (3)
]

shows the boundary map
\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(k,S)\to N^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}S

has finitely presented kernel and cokernel.
Now we use

[
Sta20
,
Tag 0519
]

to finish the proof.
∎

Proposition 7.19
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a smooth proper
p
-adic formal scheme over

\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{K})
.
The
S/p^{n}
-module
{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(\mathcal{X}_{n}/S_{n})

is finitely presented, for any integer
i
and
any
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.

Let us stress again that this already follows from
[
BS19
, Theorem 5.2]
.

Proof.

The case of finite
n
follows from
Lemma

7.15
:
the prismatic cohomology complex is a perfect complex, hence the comparison

[
BS19
, Theorem 5.2]
or
Theorem

3.5

shows the crystalline cohomology complex is also perfect
over the coherent ring
S/p^{n}
.
Therefore all of its cohomology modules are finitely presented as
S/p^{n}
-modules.

Now we turn to the case
n=\infty
.
By
Lemma

7.16
there is a short exact sequence:

0\to{\rm H}^{i}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathfrak{S})\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\varphi}S\to{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(\mathcal{X}/S)\to\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}({\rm H}^{i+1}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathfrak{S}),\varphi_{*}S)\to 0.

Since prismatic cohomology complex is a perfect complex and the ring
\mathfrak{S}

is Noetherian, we know the term
{\rm H}^{i}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathfrak{S})\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\varphi}S

is finitely presented.
Using
[
Sta20
,
Tag 0519
]

we are reduced to showing the term

\mathrm{Tor}_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}({\rm H}^{i+1}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathfrak{S}),\varphi_{*}S)

is finitely presented.
This in turn follows from
Proposition

7.17
and
the fact that
{\rm H}^{i+1}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathfrak{S})
is a Kisin module:
see
Corollary

7.4
.
∎

Now we turn to the main result of our paper,
which concerns the Breuil-module structure of the crystalline cohomology.
Write
\mathfrak{M}^{j}_{n}:={\rm H}^{j}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})
and
\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n}:={\rm H}^{j}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
.

Lemma 7.20
.

The following sequence

0\to\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}/u\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\to\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}/I^{+}S\to\Tor_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n},\varphi_{*}S)/I^{+}S\to 0

is exact.

Proof.

By derived mod
p^{n}
version of
Theorem

3.11
, we have
S\otimes_{\varphi,\mathfrak{S}}^{\mathbb L}\RG_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})\simeq\RG_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
. So Lemma
7.16
yields an exact sequence

(7.21)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S\otimes_{\varphi,\mathfrak{S}}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Tor_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n},\varphi_{*}S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

as
\varphi
on
\mathfrak{S}
is finite flat.

We only need to show the above exact sequence remains left exact after modulo
I^{+}S
. To see this, note that

\RG_{\cris}(X_{k}/W_{n}(k))\simeq\RG_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})\otimes^{\mathbb L}_{\mathfrak{S}}W(k)\simeq\RG_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})\otimes^{\mathbb L}_{S}W(k)
, where in the last identification we use the fact that Frobenius on
W(k)
is an isomorphism.
Using the exact sequence (
7.7
), then we have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{S/I^{+}S\otimes_{\varphi,\mathfrak{S}}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}/I^{+}S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Tor_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n},\varphi_{*}S_{n})/I^{+}S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}/u\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}(X_{k}/W_{n}(k))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n}[u]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Since the left column is an isomorphism, we conclude that the top row is left exact as desired.
∎

Recall in
Definition

6.23
,
{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
is defined to be a Breuil module if the quadruple

\left({\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n}),{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n},\mathcal{I}^{[i]}_{\cris}),\varphi_{i},\nabla\right)

constructed in §
5.2
is an object of

\Mod_{S,\tor}^{\varphi,i,\nabla}
.
This condition is equivalent to the triple

\left({\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n}),{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n},\mathcal{I}^{[i]}_{\cris}),\varphi_{i}\right)

being an object of
\Mod_{S,\tor}^{\varphi,i}
.

Theorem 7.22
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and assume
i\leq p-2
. Then
{\rm H}^{j}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})
has no
u
-torsion for
j=i,i+1
if and only if

{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
is an Breuil module.
When that happens we have
\underline{\mathcal{M}}({\rm H}^{i}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}))\simeq{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
inside
\textnormal{Mod}^{\varphi,i}_{S,\tor}
.

Proof.

Write
\mathfrak{M}^{j}_{n}:={\rm H}^{j}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})
. Suppose that it has no
u
-torsion for
j=i,i+1
.
So
\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}
is an étale Kisin module of height
i
by Proposition
7.2
.
By the discussion of §
6.3
, we know
\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}:=\underline{\mathcal{M}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})
is an object of
\text{Mod}^{\varphi,i}_{S,\tor}
.
By derived mod
p^{n}
version of
Theorem

3.11
, we have
S\otimes_{\varphi,\mathfrak{S}}^{\mathbb L}\RG_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})\simeq\RG_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
. So Lemma
7.16
yields

(7.23)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S\otimes_{\varphi,\mathfrak{S}}{\rm H}^{i}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Tor_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}({\rm H}^{i+1}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}),\varphi_{*}S_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Our assumption that
\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n}
has no
u
-torsion gives an isomorphism
\iota:S\otimes_{\varphi,\mathfrak{S}}{\rm H}^{j}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})\simeq{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
.
Now we claim that
\iota
induces a natural map
\iota^{i}:\Fil^{i}\underline{\mathcal{M}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})\to{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n},\mathcal{I}_{\cris}^{[i]})

and both the source and target are natural submodules inside
{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
.
In particular, the
\iota^{i}
is an injection.
To see this, we note that
\iota
is induced by natural map
\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\to\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}
which we still denote by
\iota
.
By Theorem
4.21
, we have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i-1}_{\qsyn}(X_{n},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{-/\mathfrak{S}}/\Fil^{i}_{\rm N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{-/\mathfrak{S}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X_{n},\Fil^{i}_{\rm N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{-/\mathfrak{S}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X_{n},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{-/\mathfrak{S}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i-1}_{\qsyn}(X_{n},\dR^{\wedge}_{-/\mathfrak{S}}/\Fil^{i}_{\rm H}\dR^{\wedge}_{-/\mathfrak{S}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X_{n},\Fil^{i}_{\rm H}\dR^{\wedge}_{-/\mathfrak{S}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}_{\qsyn}(X_{n},\dR^{\wedge}_{-/\mathfrak{S}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}

with both rows being exact.
By Theorem
4.21
(4), the left column is an isomorphism. As
\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}
is assumed to have no
u
-torsion,

Corollary

7.11
shows that
\beta
is an injection. Thus
\alpha
and hence
\alpha^{\prime}
are zero maps. So
\beta^{\prime}
is an injection. Therefore, Theorem
4.1
gives the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Fil^{i}_{\BK}\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n},\mathcal{I}^{[i]}_{\cris})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n}).}

Since
\iota:\underline{\mathcal{M}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}={\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
is an isomorphism and

\Fil^{i}\underline{\mathcal{M}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})
is the
S
-submodule of
\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}
generated by the image of
\Fil^{i}\varphi^{*}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}
and
\Fil^{i}S\cdot\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}
,
we see
\Fil^{i}\underline{\mathcal{M}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})\subset{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n},\mathcal{I}^{[i]}_{\cris})
via
\iota
. This shows
\iota
induces an injection

\iota:\Fil^{i}\underline{\mathcal{M}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})\hookrightarrow{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n},\mathcal{I}^{[i]}_{\cris})
.

Next we claim that
\iota^{i}
is an isomorphism.
After faithfully flat base changing along
S_{n}\to A_{\cris,n}:=A_{\cris}/p^{n}
, we are now working with
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq X_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{C}}}
.
Now we need some some facts about the sheaf
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(h)
on
\mathcal{X}_{\qsyn}
defined in
[
BMS19
, §7.4]
.
First according to
[
BMS19
, Theorem 10.1]
, we have
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}(h)\simeq\tau^{\leq h}{\rm R}\psi_{*}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}(h))
where

\psi:(\mathcal{X}_{\mathbf{C}})_{\et}\to\mathcal{X}_{\et}
is the natural map of étale sites.
By
[
AMMN20
, Theorem F]
, when
h\leq p-2
, we have

\mathbb{Z}_{p}(h)\simeq\fib(\varphi_{h}-1:\Fil^{h}_{\rm H}\dR^{\wedge}_{-/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\to\dR^{\wedge}_{-/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}).

Now
Proposition

5.4
implies

\mathbb{Z}_{p}(h)\simeq\fib(\varphi_{h}-1:\Fil^{h}_{\rm H}\dR^{\wedge}_{-/A_{\inf}}\to\dR^{\wedge}_{-/A_{\inf}}).

Since
{\rm fib}
commutes with derived mod
p^{n}
, we may apply
\otimes^{{\mathbb L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
to this equation.
Finally by Theorem
4.1
, we get the following exact sequence for
i\leq h\leq p-2
:

(7.24)

\cdots{\rm H}^{i-1}_{\cris}(\mathcal{X}_{n}/A_{\cris,n})\to{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(\mathcal{X}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}(h))\to{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(\mathcal{X}_{n}/A_{\cris,n},\mathcal{I}_{\cris}^{[h]})\overset{\varphi_{h}-1}{\longrightarrow}{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(\mathcal{X}_{n}/A_{\cris,n})

By Equation (
7.24
) and Proposition
6.12
, we obtain the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{S}(\underline{\mathcal{M}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{A_{\cris}\otimes_{S}\Fil^{i}\underline{\mathcal{M}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\otimes\iota^{i}}
\textstyle{A_{\cris}\otimes_{S}\underline{\mathcal{M}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(\mathcal{X}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})(i)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(\mathcal{X}_{n}/A_{\cris,n},\mathcal{I}_{\cris}^{[i]})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(\mathcal{X}_{n}/A_{\cris,n})}

with both rows being exact.
Since
1\otimes\iota^{i}
is an injection, we see that the map
\alpha
is also injective.
Then
\alpha
must be an isomorphism because
T_{S}(\underline{\mathcal{M}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}))\simeq T_{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})(i)\simeq{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(\mathcal{X}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})(i)

due to Proposition
6.12
and
[
BS19
, Theorem 1.8.(4)]
.
Therefore
s
is also injective. Now by the snake lemma, we see that
\coker(1\otimes\iota)=0
as required.

Conversely, assume that
\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}:={\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
is an object in
\textnormal{Mod}^{\varphi,i}_{S,\tor}
with
\Fil^{i}\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}={\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n},\mathcal{I}_{\cris}^{[i]})
. As before, we consider the base change
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq X_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{C}}}
and we still have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{S}({\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{A_{\cris}\otimes_{S}\Fil^{i}\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{A_{\cris}\otimes_{S}{\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(\mathcal{X}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})(i)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(\mathcal{X}_{n}/A_{\cris,n},\mathcal{I}_{\cris}^{[i]})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(\mathcal{X}_{n}/A_{\cris,n}).}

The difference here is that the middle column is now an isomorphism,
whereas the first column
\alpha
is not known to be an isomorphism.

First it is easy to see that
\alpha
is an injection by chasing the diagram.
Now by Corollary
6.15
, we have an inequality

\Len_{W(k)}(\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}/I^{+}S)=\Len_{\mathbb{Z}}(T_{S}(\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}))\leq\Len_{\mathbb{Z}}({\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(\mathcal{X}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})).

On the other hand, by the proof of Lemma
7.6
and Lemma
7.20
, we see that

\Len_{\mathbb{Z}}({\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(\mathcal{X}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}))\leq\Len_{W(k)}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}/u\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})\leq\Len_{W(k)}(\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}/I^{+}S).

Combining the above two inequalities, we see

\Len_{\mathbb{Z}}({\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(\mathcal{X}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}))=\Len_{W(k)}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}/u\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})=\Len_{W(k)}(\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}/I^{+}S).

Now the proof of Lemma
7.6
implies that
\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}
has no
u
-torsion.
By the length equality, the injection

\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}/u\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}/I^{+}S
in
Lemma

7.20
is in fact an isomorphism.
and hence
\Tor_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n},\varphi_{*}S)/I^{+}S=0
.
It is easy to see that
\Tor_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n},\varphi_{*}S)
is a finitely generated
S
module, applying Nakayama’s lemma
yields

\Tor_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n},\varphi_{*}S)=0
. Therefore
\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n}
has no
u
-torsion by the following claim.

Claim: If
\mathfrak{M}
is a
p^{n}
-torsion
\mathfrak{S}
-module and
\Tor_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M},\varphi_{*}S)=0
then
\mathfrak{M}
has no
u
-torsion.
To prove this, we first note that

\Tor_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(-,\varphi_{*}S)
is an left exact functor by
Lemma

7.16
.
Secondly note that
\mathfrak{M}
has no
u
-torsion if and only if it has no
(u,p)
-torsion.
Let
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\subset\mathfrak{M}
be the submodule of
(u,p)
-torsions in
\mathfrak{M}
.
The above discussion implies that
\Tor_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M}^{\prime},\varphi_{*}S)=0
.
Now by definition, we have
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\cong\oplus_{\Lambda}k
as an
\mathfrak{S}
-module, where
\Lambda
is an indexing set.
One computes directly that

\Tor_{1}^{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M}^{\prime},\varphi_{*}S)=\oplus_{\Lambda}\Tor^{1}_{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{S}/(p,u),\varphi_{*}S)=\oplus_{\Lambda}\Tor^{1}_{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{S}/(p,u^{p}),S)=\oplus_{\Lambda}\ker(S/p\xrightarrow{\cdot u^{p}}S/p).

Since
\ker(S/p\xrightarrow{\cdot u^{p}}S/p)
is nonzero (
u^{pe}=0
in
S/p
), the above computation implies

\Lambda=\emptyset
, as claimed.
∎

Corollary 7.25
.

If
ei<p-1
then
{\rm H}^{j}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})
has no
u
-torsion for
j=i,i+1
,
and
{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
is a Breuil module.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.5
and Proposition
7.2
,
we know that
{\rm H}^{i}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})
has no
u
-torsion.
To show that
{\rm H}^{i+1}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})
has no
u
-torsion, we first consider the case that
n=1
.
The main theorem of
[
Car08
]
shows that
{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{1}/S_{1})
is a Breuil module when
n=1
and
ei<p-1
.
Then
Theorem

7.22
shows that
{\rm H}^{i+1}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{1}/\mathfrak{S}_{1})
has no
u
-torsion.

Let us prove by induction that
\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n}:={\rm H}^{i+1}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})
has no
u
-torsion.
We use the long exact sequence relating various
\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n}:={\rm H}^{i+1}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})
:

\cdots\to\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n-1}\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{1}\to\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n}\to\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n-1}\cdots.

By induction, we may assume that
\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{n-1}
has no
u
-torsion. It suffices to prove that

\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{1}/f(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n-1})
has no
u
-torsion.
To that end, write
\mathfrak{N}\coloneqq f(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n-1})
which has height
i
,

\mathfrak{M}\coloneqq\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{1}
which has height
i+1
and

\mathfrak{L}\coloneqq\mathfrak{M}^{i+1}_{1}/\mathfrak{N}
.
By construction we have the following exact sequence

0\to\mathfrak{N}\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}\mathfrak{M}\overset{g}{\longrightarrow}\mathfrak{L}\to 0.

Let
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=g^{-1}(\mathfrak{L}[u^{\infty}])
. Then we obtain two exact sequences

0\to\mathfrak{N}\to\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{L}[u^{\infty}]\to 0\text{ ~and~ }0\to\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{M}\to\mathfrak{L}/\mathfrak{L}[u^{\infty}]\to 0.

The second sequence has all terms being étale Kisin modules. Since
\mathfrak{M}
has height
i+1
,
we conclude that both
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{L}/\mathfrak{L}[u^{\infty}]
has height
i+1
.
Since both
\mathfrak{N}
and
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
are étale and hence finite free over
{k[\![u]\!]}
.
This allows us to choose a basis
e_{1},\dots,e_{d}
of
\mathfrak{N}
and a basis
e^{\prime}_{1},\dots,e^{\prime}_{d}
of
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
so that
(e_{1},\dots,e_{d})=(e^{\prime}_{1},\dots,e^{\prime}_{d})\Lambda
, where
\Lambda=\mathrm{diag}(u^{a_{1}}\dots,u^{a_{d}})
a diagonal matrix with

u^{a_{j}}
on the main diagonal such that
a_{1}\leq a_{2}\leq\dots\leq a_{d}
.
Let
A
and
A^{\prime}
be the matrices of Frobenius for the corresponding basis. We easily see that following relation

\Lambda A=A^{\prime}\varphi(\Lambda).

Hence the last column of
A^{\prime}\varphi(\Lambda)
is divisible by
u^{pa_{d}}
.
Consequently, the last column of
A
is divisible by
u^{(p-1)a_{d}}
.
But
\mathfrak{N}
has height
i
, which means there exists a matrix
B
with entries in
{k[\![u]\!]}
so that
AB=BA=u^{ei}I_{d}
.
But this is impossible as
ei<p-1
,
unless
a_{d}=0
. This forces that
\Lambda=I_{d}
and hence a posteriori
\mathfrak{L}
has no
u
-torsion as desired.
∎

Remark 7.26
.

Let
T
be the largest integer satisfying
T\cdot e<p-1
, and let
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
It is a result of Min
[
Min20
, Lemma 5.1]
that
{\rm H}^{i}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X/\mathfrak{S})
has no
u
-torsion when

0\leq i\leq T+1
.
By a similar argument, one can also show that
{\rm H}^{i}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})
has no
u
-torsion for
0\leq i\leq T
.
The slight improvement along this direction in
Corollary

7.25
is the statement that
{\rm H}^{T+1}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})

is also
u
-torsion free.
This would imply Min’s result.
As far as we can tell, Min’s strategy does not give
u
-torsion freeness of
{\rm H}^{T+1}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n})
.

Proposition 7.27
.

Let
i\leq p-2
be an integer.
Suppose that
{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n},\mathcal{I}^{[i]}_{\cris})\to{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})\eqqcolon\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}

is injective, and denote its image as
\Fil^{i}\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}
.
Assume furthermore that
\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}
together with

(\Fil^{i}\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}={\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n},\mathcal{I}^{[i]}_{\cris}),\varphi_{i},\nabla)

is an object of
\textnormal{Mod}^{\varphi,i,\nabla}_{S,\tor}
.
Then
T_{S}(\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n})\simeq{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})(i)
as
G_{K}
-representations.

Proof.

Theorem
7.22
together with Proposition
6.12
have already shown the following isomorphisms

T_{S}({\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n}))\overset{\iota_{1}}{\simeq}T_{\mathfrak{S}}({\rm H}^{i}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}))(i)\overset{\iota_{2}}{\simeq}{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})(i).

The main point here is to check these two isomorphisms
\iota_{1},\iota_{2}
here are compatible with
G_{K}
-actions.
Let
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq X_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{C}}}
.

First we have
A_{\inf}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n}\simeq{\rm H}^{i}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}/A_{\inf,n})
which admits natural
G_{K}
-action.
Since
A_{\inf}
is a perfect prism,
[
BS19
, Theorem 1.8.(4)]
proves that
T_{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})=({\rm H}^{i}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}/A_{\inf,n}))^{\varphi=1}\simeq{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})
is compatible with
G_{K}
-action, as explained in Remark
7.5
.
This concludes that
\iota_{2}
is compatible with
G_{K}
-actions.

Now Theorem
3.11
shows that the comparison isomorphism

\overline{\iota}:{\rm H}^{i}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}/A_{\inf,n})\otimes_{A_{\inf},\varphi}A_{\cris}\simeq{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(\mathcal{X}_{n}/A_{\cris,n})

is functorial.
So
\overline{\iota}
is compatible with natural
G_{K}
-actions on the both sides.
Also
\overline{\iota}
is compatible with the isomorphism

\iota:\underline{\mathcal{M}}(\mathfrak{M}^{i}_{n})\simeq{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
.
Applying Remark
6.14
here then concludes that

\iota_{1}:T_{S}({\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})){\simeq}T_{\mathfrak{S}}({\rm H}^{i}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X_{n}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}))(i)

is compatible with
G_{K}
-actions
if
we define the
G_{K}
-action on
{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})\otimes_{S}A_{\cris}
via the identification

{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})\otimes_{S}A_{\cris}={\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(\mathcal{X}_{n}/A_{\cris,n})
.
Recall the
G_{K}
-action on
{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})\otimes_{S}A_{\cris}
is defined via Formula (
6.19
),
and we have showed in §
5.3
that these two
G_{K}
-actions are the same.
This proves that
\iota_{1}
is also compatible with
G_{K}
-actions.
∎

In the end of this subsection, we explain how our results are related to Fontaine–Messing theory in
[
FM87
]

(see also
[
Kat87
]
)
for a proper smooth
formal
scheme
X
over
W(k)
.
For any
n\geq 1
, the scheme
X_{n}
is smooth proper over
\Spec(W_{n}(k))
.
So when
0\leq j\leq i\leq p-1
, the triple
M^{i}:=({\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/W_{n}(k)),{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/W_{n}(k),\mathcal{I}^{[j]}_{\cris}),\varphi_{j})
is known to be a Fontaine–Laffaille data.

Now let
i\leq p-2
and one wants to show that
T_{\cris}(M^{i})\simeq{\rm H}^{i}_{\et}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})(i)
.
We recall the construction of
T_{\cris}(M^{i})
in the following: write
\Fil^{j}M^{i}:={\rm H}_{\cris}^{i}(X_{n}/W_{n}(k),\mathcal{I}^{[j]}_{\cris})
and let

\Fil^{i}(A_{\cris}\otimes_{W(k)}M^{i})=\sum^{i}_{j=0}\Fil^{j}A_{\cris}\otimes_{W(k)}\Fil^{i-j}M^{i}\subset A_{\cris}\otimes_{W(k)}M^{i}.

Then one can define

\varphi_{i}:\Fil^{i}(A_{\cris}\otimes_{W(k)}M^{i})\to A_{\cris}\otimes_{W(k)}M^{i}
by
\varphi_{i}:=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{i}\varphi_{j}|_{\Fil^{j}A_{\cris}}\otimes\varphi_{i-j}|_{\Fil^{i-j}M^{i}}
. Now

T_{\cris}(M^{i}):=(\Fil^{i}(A_{\cris}\otimes M^{i}))^{\varphi_{i}=1}
.

Let
\mathcal{M}^{i}:={\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n})
which is an object of
\Mod^{\varphi,i,\nabla}_{S,\tor}
by
Corollary

7.25
.
It is clear that the base change map
\iota:S\otimes_{W(k)}M^{i}\to\mathcal{M}^{i}
is an isomorphism as
W(k)\to S
is flat. Define

\Fil^{i}(S\otimes_{W(k)}M^{i}):=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{i}\Fil^{j}S\otimes_{W(k)}\Fil^{i-j}M^{i}\subset S\otimes_{W(k)}M^{i}
. Since
\Fil^{j}M^{i}
is direct summand of
\Fil^{j-1}M^{i}
,
the natural map
\Fil^{i}(S\otimes_{W(k)}M^{i})\to{\rm H}^{i}_{\cris}(X_{n}/S_{n},\mathcal{I}^{[i]}_{\cris})
induced by
\iota
is injective. Therefore, we obtain the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{\cris}(M^{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Fil^{i}(A_{\cris}\otimes_{W(k)}M^{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{i}-1}
\textstyle{A_{\cris}\otimes_{W(k)}M^{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{S}(\mathcal{M}^{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Fil^{i}(A_{\cris}\otimes_{S}\mathcal{M}^{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{i}-1}
\textstyle{A_{\cris}\otimes_{S}\mathcal{M}^{i}.}

It is well-known from Fontaine–Laffaille theory that
\Len_{\mathbb{Z}}T_{\cris}(M^{i})=\Len_{W(k)}M^{i}
.
By Corollary
6.15
, we know
\Len_{\mathbb{Z}}(T_{S}(\mathcal{M}^{i}))=\Len_{W(k)}(\mathcal{M}^{i}/I^{+}S)=\Len_{W(k)}M^{i}
. Therefore, the left column must be bijective. By Proposition
7.27
, it remains to check that the isomorphism
T_{\cris}(M^{i})\to T_{S}(\mathcal{M}^{i})
is compatible with
G_{K}
-actions.
Since the
G_{K}
-action on
T_{S}(\mathcal{M}^{i})
is the
G_{K}
-action on
A_{\cris}\otimes_{S}\mathcal{M}^{i}
via (
6.19
),
it suffices to show that
M^{i}\subset(\mathcal{M}^{i})^{\nabla=0}
,
which follows from Proposition
5.4
(1).

Corollary 7.28
.

Fontaine-Messing theory in
[
FM87
]
and
[
Kat87
]
accommodates
X
being proper smooth formal scheme over
W(k)
.

8.
Some calculations on
T_{S}

8.1.
Identification on (
6.20
) and (
6.19
)

In this section, we show that (
6.20
) and (
6.19
) are the same.

Lemma 8.1
.

If we write
N^{n}=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}A_{i,n}u^{i}\nabla^{i}
then
A_{i,n+1}=A_{i-1,n}+iA_{i,n}
and
A_{1,n}=A_{n,n}=1

Proof.

An easy induction on
n
by
N=u\nabla
.
∎

Recall that
\gamma_{i}(t)
denote the
i
-divided power of
t
.

Lemma 8.2
.

\sum_{n\geq i}A_{i,n}\gamma_{n}(t)=\gamma_{i}((e^{t}-1)).

Proof.

It suffices to show that Taylor’s expansion as functions of
t
on both sides are equal. This is clear to see that the coefficients of
t^{n}
, which is first nonzero term, coincide on the both sides. If we write

\gamma_{i}(e^{t}-1)=\sum_{n\geq i}B_{i,n}\gamma_{n}(t)
then it suffices to show that
B_{i,n}
satisfies the recursive formula:
B_{i,n+1}=B_{i-1,n}+iB_{i,n}
for
n\geq i
. Note that

\gamma_{i}(e^{t}-1)=\frac{1}{i!}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{i}{i\choose m}(-1)^{i-m}e^{mt}\right).

Therefore,
B_{i,n}=\frac{1}{i!}\left(\sum\limits_{m=0}^{i}{i\choose m}(-1)^{i-m}m^{n}\right)
. So to check that
B_{i-1,n}+iB_{i,n}=B_{i,n+1}
is equivalent to check that

\frac{1}{(i-1)!}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{i-1}{i-1\choose m}(-1)^{i-1-m}m^{n}+\sum_{m=0}^{i}{i\choose m}(-1)^{i-m}m^{n}\right)=\frac{1}{i!}\sum_{m=0}^{i}{i\choose m}(-1)^{i-m}m^{n+1}.

This follows that
i\left({i\choose m}-{i-1\choose m}\right)={i\choose m}m.

∎

Now by the above Lemmas,

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}N^{n}(x)\gamma_{n}(\log(\underline{\epsilon}(\sigma))=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\nabla^{n}(x)u^{n}\gamma_{n}(e^{\log(\underline{\epsilon}(\sigma))}-1)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\nabla^{n}(x)\gamma_{n}(u(\underline{\epsilon}(\sigma)-1))=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\nabla^{n}(x)\gamma_{n}(\sigma(u)-u).

This proves that (
6.20
) and (
6.19
) are the same.

8.2.
T_{S}
and
T_{\st,\star}

In this subsection, we explain our functor
T_{S}
and functor
T_{\st,\star}
used in
[
Car08
]
are the same.
For this purpose, we have to review period ring
\widehat{A}_{\st}
from
[
Car08
]
.
Let
\widehat{A}_{\st}=A_{\cris}\langle X\rangle
be the
p
-adic completion of PD algebra of
A_{\cris}
.
We extend Frobenius
\varphi
and filtration of
A_{\cris}
to
\widehat{A}_{\st}
as follows: Let
\varphi(X)=(1+X)^{p}-1
and

\Fil^{i}\widehat{A}_{\st}\coloneqq\left\{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}a_{j}\gamma_{j}(X),a_{j}\in\Fil^{\max\{i-j,0\}}A_{\cris},\lim_{j\to\infty}a_{j}\to 0\ \ p-\text{adically}\right\}.

It is easy to see that we can define
\varphi_{r}:\Fil^{r}\widehat{A}_{\st}\to\widehat{A}_{\st}
similar to that of
A_{\cris}
. To extend
G_{K}
-action to
\widehat{A}_{\st},
for any
g\in G_{K},
recall that
\underline{\varepsilon}(g)=\frac{g([\underline{\pi}])}{[\underline{\pi}]}\in A_{\inf}
defined before (
6.20
). Set
g(X)=\underline{\varepsilon}(g)X+\underline{\varepsilon}(g)-1
. Finally define an
A_{\cris}
-linear monodromy by set
N(X)=-(1+X)
. We embed
S
inside
\widehat{A}_{\st}
via
u\mapsto[\underline{\pi}](1+X)^{-1}
. At this point, we have two embeddings
S\to\widehat{A}_{\st}
:
\iota_{1}:S\hookrightarrow A_{\cris}\subset\widehat{A}_{\st}
via
u\mapsto[\underline{\pi}]\in A_{\inf}
and
\iota_{2}:S\hookrightarrow\widehat{A}_{\st}
via
u\mapsto[\underline{\pi}](1+X)^{-1}
. We will use both embeddings in the following. Notice that there is an
A_{\cris}
-linear projection
q:\widehat{A}_{\st}\to A_{\cris}
by sending
\gamma_{i}(X)\mapsto 0
. It is easy to check that
q
is compatible with filtration, Frobenius,
G_{K}
-actions, and
both
embeddings
\iota_{i}:S\hookrightarrow\widehat{A}_{\st}
. Set
\beta:=\log(1+X)\in\widehat{A}_{\st}
.

Remark 8.3
.

Breuil–Caruso’s theory set
N(1+X)=1+X
. Our setting is different by
-1
sign to fit our setting
\nabla(u)=1
. There is no difference for these two different settings up to changing some signs.

Given a Breuil module
\mathcal{M}\in\Mod_{S,\tor}^{\varphi,N,h}
, we extends filtration,
\varphi_{h}
, monodromy and
G_{K}
-actions to
\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}
as follows

\Fil^{h}\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}=\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\Fil^{h}\mathcal{M}+\Fil^{h}\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}.

For
a\otimes m\in\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\Fil^{h}\mathcal{M}
, set
\varphi_{h}(a\otimes m)=\varphi(a)\otimes\varphi_{h}(m)
, and for
a\otimes m\in\Fil^{h}\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2}S}\mathcal{M}
, set
\varphi_{h}(a\otimes m)=\varphi_{h}(a)\otimes\varphi_{h}(E^{h}m).
It is easy to check these
\varphi_{h}
are compatible with intersection so that
\varphi_{h}
extends to
\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}
.
We extend
G_{K}
-action from
\widehat{A}_{\st}
to
\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}
by acting on
\mathcal{M}
-trivially, and
N(a\otimes m)=N(a)\otimes m+a\otimes N(m),\forall a\in\widehat{A}_{\st},m\in\mathcal{M}
.
Now set

T_{\st}(\mathcal{M}):=(\Fil^{h}(\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}))^{\varphi_{h}=1,N=0}.

Proposition 8.4
.

There is an isomorphism
T_{S}(\mathcal{M})\simeq T_{\st,\star}(\mathcal{M})
as
G_{K}
-representations.

Proof.

For
m\in\mathcal{M}\subset\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}
, set

m^{\nabla}\coloneqq\sum\limits_{i=0}^{\infty}N^{i}(m)\gamma_{i}(\beta)
and
\mathcal{M}^{\nabla}=\{m^{\nabla}|m\in\mathcal{M}\}\subset\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}.
To understand the map
\alpha:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}^{\nabla}
, consider the following diagram induced by
q:\widehat{A}_{\st}\twoheadrightarrow A_{\cris}
:

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}^{\nabla}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{\cris}\otimes_{S}\mathcal{M}.}

Where
\alpha^{\prime}:\mathcal{M}^{\nabla}\to q(\mathcal{M})=\mathcal{M}
is induced by
q
. By definition of
\alpha
, it is easy to show that
\alpha
and
\alpha^{\prime}
is bijective. Also
\alpha
is an isomorphism of
S
-modules in the sense of
\alpha(\iota_{2}(s)m)=\iota_{1}(s)\alpha(m)
for
s\in S
and
m\in\mathcal{M}
. Using that
N
satisfies Griffiths transversality and diagram(
6.17
) together with facts that
\beta\in\Fil^{1}\widehat{A}_{\st}
and
\varphi(\beta)=p\beta
, a similar argument in Lemma
6.21
(by replacing
a
with
\beta
) show that for any
m\in\Fil^{h}\mathcal{M}
we have
m^{\nabla}\in\Fil^{h}(\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M})
and

\varphi_{h}(m^{\nabla})=(\varphi_{h}(m))^{\nabla}
. In summary,
\alpha:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}^{\nabla}
is an isomorphism in
\Mod_{S,\tor}^{\varphi,h}
and injections
\mathcal{M}\overset{\alpha}{\simeq}\mathcal{M}^{\nabla}\subset\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}
are compatible with with filtrations and
\varphi_{h}
.

Now consider the natural map
A_{\cris}\otimes_{S}\mathcal{M}^{\nabla}\in\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}
induced by inclusion
j:\mathcal{M}^{\nabla}\subset\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}
which is still denoted by
\widetilde{j}
. Since
q\circ\widetilde{j}
is an isomorphism (as
A_{\cris}\otimes_{S}(q\circ\alpha)
is an isomorphism), we conclude that

A_{\cris}\otimes_{S}\mathcal{M}^{\nabla}
is an
A_{\cris}
-submodule of
\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}
which is compatible with filtration and
\varphi_{h}
.

Using that
N(\beta)=-1
, we easily see that
\mathcal{M}^{\nabla}\subset(\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{S}\mathcal{M})^{N=0}
. In particular, we have an injection
\widetilde{j}:A_{\cris}\otimes_{S}M^{\nabla}\to(\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M})^{N=0}
compatible with filtration and
\varphi_{h}
.
Therefore
\widetilde{j}
induces an injection

T_{S}(\mathcal{M})=(\Fil^{h}(A_{\cris}\otimes_{S}\mathcal{M}))^{\varphi_{h}=1}\overset{\alpha}{\simeq}(\Fil^{h}(A_{\cris}\otimes_{S}\mathcal{M}^{\nabla}))^{\varphi_{h}=1}\subset(\Fil^{h}(\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}))^{\varphi_{h}=1,N=0}=T_{\st,\star}(\mathcal{M}).

To see this this injection is an isomorphism, we can dévissage to the case that
\mathcal{M}
is killed by
p
because both
T_{S}
and
T_{\st,\star}
are exact functors (
[
Car08
, Corollary 2.3.10]
). In this case, it also well-known that
\dim_{{\mathbb F}_{p}}T_{\st,\star}=\rank_{S_{1}}\mathcal{M}=\dim_{{\mathbb F}_{p}}T_{S}(\mathcal{M})
. This establish the isomorphism

T_{S}(\mathcal{M})\simeq T_{\st,\star}(\mathcal{M})
. Finally, we check this isomorphism is compatible with
G_{K}
-actions. Note that
T_{S}(\mathcal{M})
has
G_{K}
-action via (
6.20
), while
T_{\st,\star}
has
G_{K}
-action from that on
\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}
with trivial
G_{K}
-action on
\mathcal{M}
. We have to show that
A_{\cris}\otimes_{S}\mathcal{M}^{\nabla}
has
G_{K}
-action as the subspace of
\widehat{A}_{\st}\otimes_{\iota_{2},S}\mathcal{M}
is the same as that defined as (
6.20
). But this easily follows from the formula that
m^{\nabla}\coloneqq\sum\limits_{i=0}^{\infty}N^{i}(m)\gamma_{i}(\beta)
and
g(\beta)=\log(\underline{\varepsilon}(g))+\beta
.
∎
